- E-544 Opening-range midpoint hold: The midpoint of the opening range is the opening auction's fair value; sustained closes beyond it after the range completes show which side won the opening and tend to continue.
- E-545 Volume-trough bar break: A bar with the lowest volume in a while means resting liquidity thinned out; the next bar that breaks its high or low finds little opposition and follows through.
- E-546 Opening-volume-backed range break: A session whose opening range traded much more volume than recent opening ranges has strong participation; a break of that range is backed by real flow and continues.
- E-547 Momentum hook after an extreme: After an outsized counter move, momentum turning back through zero marks forced liquidations ending; trade the turn.
- E-548 Multi-horizon stretch agreement fade: When the 1x, 2x and 4x horizon returns are all stretched the same way in volatility units, late chasers have pushed the index beyond what liquidity providers need; the next 30 minutes tend to give part back.
- E-549 Persistence-regime bar-break follow: When the rolling lag-1 return autocorrelation is positive (orders are being sliced and executed in one direction), a close beyond the previous bar's extreme is more likely to continue than to bounce.
- E-550 Clock-block colour streak exhaustion fade: After several consecutive clock-aligned blocks of the same colour, the participants who wanted that direction are done; fade the move at the block close that completes the streak.
- E-551 Schaff-style double-stochastic MACD turn follow: Stochastic of a stochastic of the MACD gives an early, bounded cycle signal; a cross out of the low zone marks the start of a push that momentum followers extend.
- E-552 Gap-bar reversal toward the prior close: A bar that opens far from the previous close (stops or a large market order) and closes back against the gap shows the impulse had no follow-up; the gap tends to fill.
- E-553 Efficiency-collapse trend-end fade: A very efficient (one-way) run followed by a collapse of the path-efficiency ratio means the one-sided flow has stopped; price tends to revert toward the middle of the run.
- E-554 TRIX zero-cross follow: The one-bar rate of change of a triple-smoothed EMA filters out noise; its zero cross marks a persistent change of direction that trend followers act on.
- E-555 Block-end extreme close fade: A clock block that is wide for its length and closes at its own extreme was driven by late urgency; the next block usually retraces part of it.
- E-556 Range-top dwell then slip fade: After price has sat at the top (bottom) of its rolling range for several minutes, the first clear slip back shows buyers (sellers) gave up; the dwell was distribution, so follow the slip.
- E-557 Single-bar VWAP slice follow: One large bar that opens on one side of the session VWAP and closes on the other, on above-average volume, is an institutional shift of the day's average price; follow it.
- E-559 Same-minute return sign persistence: Recurring clients (hedges, index rebalancers, ADR arbitrage) trade the same time window every day; if the trailing move into this minute had the same sign in most of the last 20 sessions, follow that sign.
- E-560 Rolling-window VWAP stretch fade: Liquidity takers who push price far from the last hour's volume-weighted fair value pay to do so; the dealers who absorb it revert price toward the rolling VWAP.
- E-561 Bar-gap pressure follow: A bar that opens above the previous close means the book was swept by aggressive market buys between prints; a run of such gap-ups shows one-sided urgency that continues.
- E-562 Afternoon-anchored VWAP stretch fade: Afternoon participants start from a fresh cost basis; a stretch from the VWAP anchored at midday, measured against its own typical stretch, is a late chase that reverts.
- E-563 Chaikin money-flow thrust follow: When bars close near their highs (or lows) on volume repeatedly, the volume-weighted close location shows persistent buying (selling) pressure that carries on.
- E-564 Return-volume correlation follow: When volume rises with up-moves (and falls with pullbacks) the move is driven by informed demand and continues; positive return-volume correlation plus a net move gives the side.
- E-565 Day-open recross follow: The day's open is the price where the overnight imbalance resolved; after a long stay on one side, the first close through it shows the other side has taken control.
- E-566 Trend-significance follow: A statistically significant slope of price over the window (t-stat of the regression) separates a real directional flow from noise; follow when the t-statistic first crosses the threshold.
- E-567 Median-anchored range stretch fade: The rolling median ignores the burst prints that drag a mean; a close several average bar-ranges away from the median is an overshoot by impatient flow and reverts.
- E-568 Seasonal-path deviation fade: Price tends to follow an average intraday path (same minute, last sessions); when the day's move strays from that path by more than its usual size, the stray is liquidity-driven and reverts toward the path.
- E-569 Day-open cross follow: After staying on one side of the session open for a while, a close across it shows the morning's positioning has been overturned; trapped traders add fuel to the move.
- E-570 Autocorrelation regime bar trade: When recent one-bar returns are negatively autocorrelated the tape is mean-reverting (fade the large bar); when positively, it is trending (follow it).
- E-571 US-cash-open anchor follow: From the US cash open, order flow from S&P-linked investors moves WIN; a move from the anchor that is larger than usual for the clock time is information-driven and keeps going.
- E-572 Clean-side micro-trend break follow: If the price has stayed almost entirely on one side of VWAP (few side flips), institutions are working an order on that side; a break of the last few minutes' extreme in that direction continues.
- E-573 Prior-day midpoint cross follow: The previous session's range midpoint is a fair-value reference; a close across it after a spell on the other side shifts acceptance and follows through.
- E-574 Same-minute drift persistence: Recurring flows (rebalancing, hedging, US data windows) push price the same way at the same clock time; trade the sign of the average forward 30-minute return when it is statistically distinct.
- E-575 Body-through-VWAP bar follow: A single large-bodied bar that opens on one side of VWAP and closes on the other is an aggressive repricing through the benchmark algos trade against; follow it.
- E-576 Heavy-open-volume range break follow: A heavy first window of volume relative to recent sessions signals large participants with a view; a break of that window's range afterward follows the aggressor.
- E-577 Bar-colour imbalance without progress fade: Many bars of one colour but little net displacement means the aggressive side is being absorbed by passive limit orders; fade the colour majority.
- E-578 Thin-volume micro-break fade: A poke beyond the recent high/low made on volume below normal for the minute of day has no real participation behind it; liquidity takers are absent and the poke is rejected back into the range.
- E-579 Tweezer reversal after a run: Two adjacent bars of opposite colour stopping at the same extreme after a run show a level that two waves of aggressive orders both failed to pass; fade it.
- E-580 Staircase stall fade: After a run of bars each making higher highs and higher lows, the first bar that fails to extend and closes down shows the late trend followers have run out of buyers.
- E-581 Open-chop micro-break fade: When price has repeatedly crossed the day's opening price in the last 90 minutes the market is rotating around a fair value; short-window breaks in that state are stop runs that revert.
- E-582 Afternoon micro-break fade: In the afternoon, after the day's information is priced and before the closing flow, short-window breaks lack follow-through and are faded by liquidity providers.
- E-583 Close-location saturation fade: When bars keep closing at one end of their range over a window, aggressors have been paying the spread for a while; that pressure is spent and the next move is back.
- E-584 Narrow-poke break fade: A break of the recent extreme made by a small-range bar is a creep rather than an impulse; a creep into resting orders gets absorbed instead of continuing.
- E-585 One-colour bar share fade: When nearly all bars in a window have the same colour, the move is one-sided enough that dip buyers or rally sellers are waiting; fade the saturation point.
- E-586 Local-median stretch fade: A close far from its rolling median, scaled by the mean absolute deviation around that median, is a robust measure of overextension that single spikes do not distort; fade it.
- E-587 Early-session micro-break fade: Right after the open, quotes are wide and breaks of the first few minutes' extremes are often liquidity vacuums, which refill and revert.
- E-588 Mean-of-daily-highs/lows band poke fade: The average of the last n sessions' highs (lows) is a level where recent days' rallies (dips) typically ended; a bar that pokes through it and closes back is a failed push into resting supply/demand; fade it.
- E-589 Zero-lag EMA slope turn follow: A zero-lag EMA turns before a plain EMA; when its slope turns with price on the same side, short-horizon momentum followers pile in for a few more bars.
- E-590 Chande momentum extreme fade: When nearly all price change in the window has been one-directional (CMO beyond a threshold) and it first relaxes, the one-sided flow is fading and the pullback side takes over; trade the relaxation.
- E-591 Relative volatility index cross follow: Volatility concentrated in up-closes (or down-closes) shows where the large flow sits; follow when the relative volatility index moves beyond a threshold.
- E-592 Piercing line / dark-cloud cover at a window extreme: A bar that reverses more than a fraction of the previous opposite body right at a window extreme shows aggressive counter flow absorbing the move; trade the reversal.
- E-593 EMA ribbon alignment onset follow: When four EMAs (u, 2u, 3u, 5u bars) first line up in order with a minimum spread, trend followers at all horizons agree; the first aligned bar is an entry in that direction.
- E-594 Stochastic RSI hook out of the extreme: RSI at the bottom (top) of its own recent range then rising (falling) back through the threshold marks a momentum turn inside the window; trade the turn.
- E-595 Prior-session projected-range level fade: The projected high/low computed from the previous session's open, high, low and close (DeMark range projection) is a level watched by a minority of traders; a poke that closes back is fading.
- E-596 Stochastic centre-of-gravity turn: The recency-weighted centre of gravity of prices turns before price itself; when it turns out of the extreme of its own window, the swing in that window is ending.
- E-597 Price-volume-trend EMA cross follow: Cumulating volume times return weighs moves by participation; the PVT crossing its EMA means volume is now backing the new direction.
- E-598 Dual-EMA cross follow: A fast EMA crossing a slower one inside the session marks the point where recent buyers or sellers outweigh the earlier ones; late counter-traders get stopped and the move extends.
- E-599 Lower-high streak break: After several consecutive lower highs sellers are leaning on every rally; the first close above the last high squeezes them (mirror for higher lows).
- E-600 Day-open recross after an excursion: After trading away from the day's open the price closes back through it: the open drive failed and the other side owns the session; follow the recross.
- E-601 New-extreme count pressure: When most recent bars keep making higher highs rather than lower lows, participation is one-sided; follow the crossing of that count.
- E-602 Bollinger re-entry fade: A close outside the Bollinger band followed by a close back inside shows the stretch was rejected; chasers are trapped and the price reverts toward the mean.
- E-603 Autocorrelation-regime bar trade: When consecutive returns have recently been negatively correlated, a big bar overshoots and is faded; when positively correlated, it is followed.
- E-604 Volatility-normalised drift follow: A window return large relative to its own noise (t-stat of drift) reflects persistent one-way order flow rather than random jitter; follow when it first crosses.
- E-605 RSI midline cross follow: RSI crossing 50 (with an optional dead band) shows average gains overtaking average losses; momentum traders pile in.
- E-606 Close-range squeeze break: When the closing-price range of the last window is much narrower than that of four windows, the market is coiled; a close outside it releases stop and breakout orders.
- E-607 TRIX zero-cross follow: The slope of a triple-smoothed EMA changing sign filters noise and signals a durable change in direction; follow it.
- E-608 Cumulative lower/upper wick pressure follow: Over a window, lower wicks much larger than upper wicks mean dips were repeatedly bought by passive liquidity that keeps absorbing; follow that side when the net wick share crosses a threshold.
- E-609 Session-midpoint dwell reclaim follow: After the price spent a while on one side of the developing day-range midpoint, the first close back across it marks the balance area being re-accepted and rotation usually continues across; follow.
- E-610 Clock-hour open dwell reclaim follow: Hourly opens are anchors for desks that scale by the hour; after staying on one side of this hour's open, a close back across it shows flow from that hour's participants switching; follow.
- E-611 Volume-weighted close vs mean close follow: When the volume-weighted mean close of a window sits above its simple mean, contracts traded at the higher prices: informed size is buying up; follow when the standardised gap crosses a threshold.
- E-612 Narrowest-range bar break (Crabel NR-n): A bar with the smallest range of the last n bars shows participants stepping aside; the next bar closing beyond its extreme releases stored energy in that direction; follow.
- E-613 Afternoon trend-day EMA-cross fade: After a morning that moved more than usual, afternoon liquidity is thin and trend followers have already positioned; a close back through a medium EMA against the day shows exhaustion; fade the day.
- E-614 Session volume-climax bar fade: The largest-volume bar of the session so far, with volume well above normal for the minute, is where the impatient side exhausts itself; the next move reverts against that bar's colour.
- E-615 Prior-session VWAP reclaim follow: Yesterday's final VWAP is the average price of the whole day's inventory; after trading on one side of it for a while, a close back across means yesterday's holders are in or out of the money again; follow.
- E-616 Return versus same minute yesterday follow: Price versus its level at the same clock minute of the previous session measures the speed of the day-over-day drift that funds benchmark against; follow when the gap exceeds its usual size.
- E-617 Midday box break follow: The 12:00 lunch window is a low-liquidity balance; the first close beyond its range after the box ends shows afternoon (US-open) flow taking control; follow the break.
- E-618 Dollar-lead lag on the index: WIN and WDO trade inversely and index futures react a bit later to a dollar move; when the dollar moves more than usual and WIN has not yet followed inversely, late index participants catch up.
- E-619 Session-open price reclaim: The session's opening print is a reference many traders watch; after trading on one side of it for a while, a close back across it shifts control.
- E-620 Bollinger squeeze release: When closing-price dispersion is far below its usual size for the time of day, stops cluster just outside the box; a close beyond the box releases the compressed volatility.
- E-621 Wick absorption balance: Persistent long lower wicks over a window show passive buyers absorbing sells; a green bar after it means the sellers are spent (mirror for upper wicks).
- E-622 Volume-climax reversal: A very heavy bar that pokes to a new window extreme but closes away from it is a capitulation or exhaustion; the forced side is done, fade it.
- E-623 EMA stretch fade in range units: Price stretched several average bar-ranges away from its short EMA is an overshoot from impatient market orders; fade the first bar that gets there.
- E-624 Quiet shock bar fade: A bar with a much larger range than usual but below-median volume moved on thin liquidity, not on real participation; fade its direction.
- E-625 Range-expansion triplet follow: Three same-colour bars with growing ranges mean participation is building in one direction; the next wave of followers keeps it going.
- E-626 Single bar through VWAP: One big-body bar that opens on one side of VWAP and closes on the other is an institutional-size push through the day's fair value; follow it.
- E-627 Previous-close line reclaim: The prior session settlement-like close is a reference for overnight positions; after the price stays on one side a while, a close across it shows those holders giving up or doubling down.
- E-628 Joint index-and-dollar move fade: The index and the dollar normally move in opposite directions; when both rise (or fall) strongly together the index move lacks its usual macro backing and the unusual co-move tends to unwind.
- E-629 Return-skewness extreme fade: Strong skew of recent 1-bar returns means a few jump bars dominated a window; the jump is liquidity driven and partly reverts.
- E-630 Kaufman adaptive average cross follow: KAMA slows down in noise and speeds up in efficient moves, so a close crossing it by a margin marks a trend the noise filter accepts; follow it.
- E-631 Donchian midline cross follow: The midpoint of the N-minute high-low channel is the balance level; a close crossing it shifts the side holding the recent range and trend followers join.
- E-632 Fisher transform extreme hook: The Fisher transform stretches the tails of the price position in its range; a turn out of a strong extreme shows exhaustion of a push, so trade the hook.
- E-633 Elder-ray pullback in a rising EMA: In a trending EMA, bear (bull) power dipping deeply and turning marks the end of a pullback by counter-trend traders; resume the trend.
- E-634 Vortex indicator gap cross follow: Vortex compares upward and downward travel against true range; a clear gap between the two shows one side owning the movement, and followers extend it.
- E-635 Ichimoku tenkan-kijun cross follow: Midpoints of two high-low windows are robust to closes; the short one crossing the long one shows the recent range shifting direction.
- E-636 Awesome oscillator saucer: Inside a positive (negative) AO regime, two weakening bars followed by a strengthening one show momentum pausing and resuming; trade the resumption.
- E-637 Volatility-ratio jump follow: When short-term volatility jumps relative to the last hour, new information is arriving; the direction of the short move is followed before it is fully priced.
- E-638 Straight-line move fade: A very efficient (near straight-line) move far larger than usual leaves late chasers with no pullback cushion; liquidity providers fade it.
- E-639 Higher-low run structure break: A run of rising lows is a staircase of dip buyers; the first close under the last low strands them and the move follows (mirror for lower highs).
- E-640 Range-expansion onset follow: When recent bar ranges jump relative to the last hour, new information or forced flow is arriving; follow the net direction of the burst.
- E-641 Rising-volume close ramp follow: Consecutive same-colour bars each on more volume than the last show aggression building; follow.
- E-642 Wide outside-bar close follow: A wide bar engulfing the prior range that closes near one end settles the auction in that direction.
- E-643 Stalled-run exhaustion fade: After a run of same-direction closes, a bar with a tiny body shows the aggressor ran out of volume; fade it.
- E-644 VWAP slope flip with price side: When the session VWAP turns up while price is above it, the average cost of the day's volume is rising with price: buyers are in control; mirror for sells.
- E-645 Double-EMA slope turn follow: A double-exponential average removes most lag; its slope turning marks a trend change earlier than a single EMA cross.
- E-646 Contracting-range break follow: Several bars of strictly shrinking range mean participants are withdrawing; the first close beyond the last bar releases stored order flow.
- E-647 VWAP-opposed close-extreme fade: A new short-term close high while price is below the day's VWAP is a rally into trapped-buyer supply; fade it (mirror above VWAP).
- E-648 Qstick body-sum follow: A rolling mean of candle bodies large relative to the mean range shows persistent one-sided aggression that is slow to be absorbed; follow the onset.
- E-649 Smoothed balance-of-power extreme fade: When the smoothed (close-open)/(high-low) ratio is extreme, one side has pushed every bar to the edge; late chasers are exposed and the move tends to give back.
- E-650 KST-lite multi-horizon ROC zero cross: A weighted sum of four rate-of-change horizons crossing zero marks a trend change that several time scales of participants agree on; follow it.
- E-651 Zigzag reversal confirmation follow: After a swing is confirmed by a retrace of a fixed fraction of price, the reversal has trapped the previous trend's late entrants; follow the new swing direction.
- E-652 Rate-of-change acceleration onset: When momentum is positive and its change over the same horizon turns positive too, buying pressure is building, not fading; follow the onset of acceleration.
- E-653 Elder impulse colour onset: Slope of an EMA and slope of the MACD histogram both rising means trend and momentum agree; the first bar of agreement is an entry for trend followers.
- E-654 Triangular moving average slope turn: A doubly smoothed average filters bar noise; when its slope changes sign the underlying drift has changed and positions of slower participants lag behind.
- E-655 Hull average slope turn: The Hull average reacts with little lag; a slope turn catches turns early, before slower averages, so slower trend followers become the fuel.
- E-656 Up-close share thrust follow: When nearly all recent closes are higher than the previous close, buy orders arrive steadily (metered execution of a large order); follow the first bar the share crosses the threshold.
- E-657 Range-position hook out of the extreme: After the close sits in the lowest (highest) slice of the rolling range and then lifts out of it while still in the lower (upper) half, the extreme was rejected; fade back toward the middle.
- E-658 Classic pivot R/S rejection: Floor-trader pivot levels (R1/S1, R2/S2) from the previous session are watched by many desks; a poke through one that closes back inside means resting orders absorbed the push, so fade it.
- E-659 Round-number rejection: Index round numbers (every 500/1000 points) hold clustered limit orders and option-strike hedging; a bar that reaches the next round level and closes back below it (or mirror) is rejected supply/demand.
- E-660 Previous clock-block range break: The range of the previous 15/30/60-minute clock block is a reference for desks that schedule by the clock; a close beyond it in the next block shows that block's balance being rejected; follow.
- E-661 Hour-turn continuation: Funds and execution algos rebalance at the top of the hour; when the move into the hour is larger than usual, the first bar of the new hour keeps the direction of that flow; follow.
- E-662 Same-minute seasonal drift: If over the last 20 sessions the next 30 minutes from this clock minute drifted one way on average and in the median, recurring scheduled flow (opens, data, rebalancing) may repeat; trade that drift.
- E-663 Weak-volume new extreme fade: A new window high/low printed on below-normal volume for the minute lacks participation; it is a liquidity poke, not acceptance, and tends to revert.
- E-664 Previous-range exhaustion reversal: When the day has already travelled a set fraction of yesterday's whole range from the open, the typical daily range budget is spent; a bar closing against the move starts the give-back.
- E-665 VWAP deviation z-score fade: Distance from VWAP scaled by its own session dispersion marks statistically stretched prices; market-order chasers overshoot and liquidity providers pull price back toward the day's fair value.
- E-666 Previous-day midpoint reclaim: The midpoint of yesterday's range is the balance point of the previous auction; after dwelling on one side, a close across it means the market re-accepts the other half; follow.
- E-667 Block close-location persistence: A clock block that closes in the top (bottom) slice of its range leaves one side unfilled; when the next block opens with a bar in the same direction, the unfilled orders continue the move; follow.
- E-668 Round-number level rejection: Stop and limit orders cluster at round index/dollar levels; a bar that pokes through a round level and closes back on the near side has run those stops without follow-through, so fade it.
- E-669 Three-bar low/high sweep reversal: A bar that sweeps below the two previous lows and closes above the previous high traps sellers; buy it (mirror for sells), only when the bar is not tiny.
- E-670 Narrowest-range bar breakout: When the previous bar has the smallest range of the last n bars, volatility is compressed; a close beyond its high/low starts the expansion in that direction.
- E-671 Floor-pivot cross with EMA side: The previous session's pivot (H+L+C)/3 is a reference many desks use; a fresh cross of it that agrees with the short EMA side signals a bias shift for the session.
- E-672 Day-open retest bounce: After the session has traded well away from its opening price, the first retest of the open is a level of unfinished business; buyers/sellers who drove the move defend it, so follow the original side.
- E-674 Close-location volume flow cross: Volume weighted by where each bar closes in its range measures who finished each bar in control; a cross of the n-bar flow above a threshold marks aggressive persistent buying (or selling).
- E-675 Hour-end drift follow: Index and dollar flow tied to hourly rebalancing and algorithmic hourly benchmarks tends to push in the direction of the hour's move into its last 10 minutes; trade with it at minute :50.
- E-676 Range-midline cross follow: The midpoint of the recent high-low range is the balance point of a Donchian channel; closing across it after trading on the other side shows supply or demand has taken over.
- E-677 Tweezer reversal after a push: Two successive bars of opposite colour stopping at the same high (or low) after a push show a defended level; the pushers are rejected twice, so fade.
- E-678 Vortex indicator cross: VI+ over VI- measures which side's bar-to-bar excursions dominate; a cross with a margin marks directional control that trend followers and stop orders extend.
- E-679 MFI oversold/overbought exit: Volume-weighted RSI leaving an extreme means forced sellers/buyers are exhausted; take the reversal.
- E-680 KAMA slope turn: The adaptive average ignores noise and only turns when efficient movement appears; a turn with price on the new side signals a new directional leg.
- E-681 Elder-ray pullback turn: In a rising EMA, bear power (low minus EMA) turning up from below shows sellers failing to push under the trend average; buy the turn (mirror for sells).
- E-682 Regression slope t-stat break: A statistically strong linear trend over a window (large slope t-stat) means persistent one-sided flow; follow its first crossing of the threshold.
- E-683 Heavy-volume rolling-VWAP cross: A cross of the rolling VWAP on above-typical volume, with the VWAP itself sloping that way, means the recent average cost basis is being reclaimed by active participants.
- E-684 EMA ribbon full alignment: When price and three geometric EMAs line up in order, short, medium and long horizons agree; the first bar of alignment starts a trend leg.
- E-685 Converging triangle break: Lower highs and higher lows with a shrinking range compress resting orders at both edges; the close beyond the recent half-window edge releases it.
- E-686 Heavy-volume doji absorption break: A high-volume bar with a tiny body means large orders absorbed the flow without moving price; a close beyond its range shows which side was absorbed and follows.
- E-688 Autocorrelation regime switch: When recent bar-to-bar returns are positively autocorrelated the last bar's direction tends to persist (trend regime, stop-chasing flow); when negatively autocorrelated it bounces (inventory-driven chop); follow or fade accordingly.
- E-689 Accumulated wick pressure: Repeated long lower wicks over a window show passive buyers absorbing sell orders (and upper wicks the reverse); when the average wick imbalance crosses a threshold, trade with the absorbers.
- E-690 Absorption bar break: A high-volume, tiny-range bar means aggressive flow met a large passive order; the next close beyond that bar shows which side gave up, and the move follows.
- E-691 Low-volume shock fade: A bar move many times larger than usual for the time of day but on ordinary volume is likely a thin-book air pocket or a single sweep; liquidity returns and the move retraces.
- E-692 Regression-channel snap-back: Price stretching more than k residual deviations from a rolling least-squares line is an overshoot against the local trend; liquidity providers fade it back toward the line.
- E-693 Session-midpoint cross follow: After the early session, a close that crosses from one half of the day's range to the other signals the balance of the day shifting; follow the cross.
- E-694 Range-expansion thrust: When the last n minutes' range is a multiple of the preceding n minutes' range and the close sits at the extreme, volatility has switched on with a directional push and stops feed it.
- E-695 Late-day VWAP magnet: Into the final stretch, institutions benchmarked to VWAP and day traders closing positions pull a stretched price back toward the session VWAP; fade the stretch.
- E-696 Prior-range-scaled thrust: A move over the last minutes that is a set fraction of yesterday's whole range is large in a way participants anchored on yesterday's levels notice; chasing flow extends it.
- E-697 Opening-range measured-move fade: Technical traders project the opening range height beyond its edge as a profit target; price reaching that projection meets take-profit orders and fades.
- E-698 Volume-fading run fade: A run of same-colour bars each on less volume than the one before is a push with no new participants; the other side absorbs it, so fade the run.
- E-699 Day-open extension fade: Distance from the session open beyond what is typical for that minute of day marks a stretched move that liquidity providers fade back toward the open.
- E-700 Squeeze false-release fade: After a volatility squeeze, a first close outside the band is often a stop-run; fade it back inside.
- E-701 Lunch-hour VWAP stretch fade: In the thin midday session stretches away from VWAP lack follow-through and revert as institutions benchmark to VWAP.
- E-702 Prior-close extension rollover: After a big extension from yesterday's close, a retrace of a large part of it shows the late participants are trapped; follow the move back toward the prior close.
- E-703 EMA distance z-score fade: A price far from its EMA in units of its own recent deviation is statistically stretched; fade back to the mean.
- E-704 Morning extreme afternoon poke rejection: The morning high/low is a level that resting orders defend; an afternoon poke beyond it that closes back inside is a failed breakout.
- E-705 Effort without result break: Rising volume while price stays in a narrow range means a large player is working an order; the break of that range shows the direction of the size.
- E-706 Same-minute spike fade: A one-bar return many times larger than typical for that minute is usually a thin-book overshoot; fade it.
- E-707 Hourly VWAP stretch fade: Traders and algos benchmark by clock hour; a price stretched from the current hour's VWAP late in the hour reverts as the hour rolls.
- E-708 Dollar-led catch-up: WIN and the dollar are inversely linked intraday; when the dollar moves sharply and the index has not yet responded, slower index participants reprice and WIN catches up.
- E-709 Day-open retest hold: After moving well away from the session open, a return to the open that closes back on the trend side shows the open acting as support/resistance for the day's inventory.
- E-710 Volatility squeeze release: Realised volatility far below its 20-session norm means stored-up orders; the first break of the recent range after the squeeze starts a directional expansion.
- E-711 Afternoon-window range break: The range built in a late-morning or midday window holds resting orders at its edges; a close outside it later in the session triggers stops and follow-through.
- E-712 Rolling volume-weighted mean fade: Price stretched far from the volume-weighted mean of the last hour has been pushed by few participants and tends to revert toward where most volume traded.
- E-713 Range thrust from the opposite side: A fast move from the lower half of the recent range to its top (or reverse) within ten minutes shows aggressive one-sided flow that continues.
- E-714 ATR stretch from EMA fade: A close several average ranges away from a short EMA is an extension that liquidity takers cannot sustain; fade it back.
- E-715 Prior-close cross after a stay: The previous settlement-like close is a reference that market participants anchor to; a first close across it after a stay on one side signals the day's sentiment flipping.
- E-716 Autocorrelation-regime bar: When the last hour's bar-to-bar returns are positively autocorrelated a big bar continues; when negatively autocorrelated it reverts. Trade the big bar according to the regime.
- E-717 Donchian midline cross: The midpoint of the recent high-low channel separates buyer and seller control; a close crossing it marks a shift in control that persists.
- E-718 Absorption bar after a run: A heavy-volume bar with a tiny body after a directional run means aggressive orders were absorbed by passive size; the run's participants are trapped, so fade it.
- E-719 Round-number bounce: Index points at round multiples attract resting orders and option hedging; a bar that dips to the level and closes clear above it (or the mirror) is a defended level.
- E-720 Day-open recross continuation: After the day traded well on one side of its open, a close back through the open means the day's early buyers/sellers lost control; follow the cross.
- E-721 Wick skew flow: Over a window, long lower tails versus upper tails show dip buying being absorbed; follow the side whose tails dominate.
- E-722 Keltner channel break follow: A close beyond EMA plus k ATR is a volatility expansion that stop orders and trend followers extend.
- E-723 Keltner channel re-entry fade: A close that stretched beyond the channel and returns inside marks an exhausted push; fade back toward the mean.
- E-724 Bandwidth squeeze break: When n-bar price dispersion is low versus its same-minute norm, liquidity providers are crowded and a break of the window range releases pent-up flow.
- E-725 Typical-price z thrust: A typical-price z-score crossing a threshold shows a statistically unusual push relative to the recent window; momentum traders continue it.
- E-726 MFI exit from extreme zone: Money-flow ratio leaving an extreme zone shows one-sided volume has dried up, so price tends to revert.
- E-727 Stair-step highs and lows: Consecutive bars each making higher highs and higher lows (or lower both) is an orderly trend that late participants chase; follow the m-th step.
- E-728 Open-dwell share dip entry: When most of the session's bars closed on one side of the day open, the day is a one-sided auction; a dip through a short EMA that closes back on the dominant side is cheap continuation for the trend participants.
- E-729 Early-extreme survival VWAP cross: If the day's high (low) formed early and the opposite extreme formed later, the day is a trend day; after the morning, a close through VWAP in the trend direction means trapped early buyers (sellers) give up.
- E-730 VWAP cross confirmed by the other market's side: A cross of the own VWAP is more reliable when the other contract (usually negatively correlated) sits on its opposite side of its short EMA: both markets then express the same risk flow.
- E-731 Day-range position divergence versus the other market: Index and dollar usually share inverse range positions inside the day. When the index sits far lower in its day range than the dollar implies, the laggard converges.
- E-732 Up-bar versus down-bar range balance follow: When the range printed by up bars dominates the range printed by down bars over a window, buyers cause the wider moves and the imbalance persists.
- E-733 Higher-high versus lower-low frequency balance: A window with many bars making higher highs and few making lower lows shows one side continually extending the price; the first time the balance gets lopsided marks control.
- E-734 V-turn after a sharp opposite move: A large move in the opposite direction of the one just before it, both beyond what is typical, signals that the earlier side was absorbed; follow the turn.
- E-735 Busy-volume pace with VWAP and open side: When cumulative session volume runs well ahead of its typical pace, participation is unusually high; price on the same side of VWAP and day open carries conviction that persists.
- E-736 Mid-range recovery measured in average daily range: Price that sits at least a fraction of the typical daily range away from both session extremes is in the middle of an expanded day; the VWAP side then gives the continuation direction.
- E-737 Volume-peak bar failure: The highest-volume bar of a window shows where aggressive participants committed; a close beyond the extreme of that bar against its direction traps them and forces exits.
- E-748 Dollar-led index catch-up: WDO and WIN are inversely linked; when the dollar makes an unusual move and the index has not yet responded by half, slower index participants catch up in the opposite direction.
- E-750 Volatility compression range break: When bar-to-bar volatility falls well below its recent norm, stops pile up around the small range; the first close beyond it releases the energy.
- E-751 Shock-bar extreme follow-through: After a wide-range full-body bar, a next close beyond its extreme shows no one absorbed the move; late traders chase.
- E-752 Late-session day-open cross: In the last hour, a close crossing the day's open decides the winners and losers of the day; position squaring pushes price the same way into the close.
- E-753 VWAP sigma-band re-entry fade: A stretch beyond a deviation band around VWAP draws in value buyers; the first close back inside the band means the push is exhausted, so trade back toward VWAP.
- E-754 Double marubozu follow: Two consecutive almost wickless bars the same way show an aggressor taking liquidity at every price; the move continues for a while.
- E-755 Absorption at the range edge: Heavy volume with little range at the edge of the last half hour means passive orders absorbed the aggressors; fade back into the range.
- E-756 Autocorrelation regime switch: When recent returns are negatively autocorrelated (noise from liquidity takers) a big bar is faded; when positively autocorrelated (herding) it is followed.
- E-757 Weak-volume new extreme fade: A new session high or low made on below-average volume lacks participation; it is a probe that tends to retrace.
- E-758 Rolling-median cross with rising median: The median of recent closes ignores spikes; a close crossing a sloping median means the typical price level is migrating, so stop-based traders chase the direction.
- E-759 VWAP chop then break: Many crossings of the VWAP mean balanced trade; a close far from VWAP after the chop signals the balance resolving and trapped counter-traders adding to the move.
- E-760 Efficiency-ratio trend onset: When net movement becomes a large share of the path travelled, flow is one-sided; follow the first bar where the Kaufman efficiency ratio crosses the threshold.
- E-761 Widest bar of the session so far: A bar wider than every earlier bar of the day, closing at its extreme in its own colour, shows the strongest aggression of the session; the other side is forced to cover.
- E-762 Same-colour big-body burst count: Several large same-direction bodies in a short window with none against show persistent institutional execution that slices orders; follow the burst.
- E-763 Early-bias VWAP side follow: A session whose first bars were mostly one colour has a directional bias; after the opening window, the first close on the bias side of VWAP is a dip-buy/rally-sell by trend participants.
- E-764 MACD histogram early turn: A histogram that rises for several bars while still below zero shows selling momentum fading before the lines cross; the early turn catches the reversal ahead of lagging followers.
- E-765 CCI re-entry fade: A Commodity Channel Index excursion beyond a large threshold followed by a return inside it marks exhausted extension; fade back toward the mean.
- E-766 Choppiness-index trend onset: A fall of the choppiness index below its trending threshold means ranges stop overlapping; follow the net direction of the window.
- E-767 Force-index thrust: Price change times volume, smoothed, beyond a typical-size threshold shows volume-backed pressure; follow the first bar where it exceeds that size.
- E-768 Time-of-day drift persistence: At a given minute of the day the next bar has leaned the same way on most of the last 20 sessions (scheduled flows: fixings, futures-roll hedges, foreign desks); trade the lean when its t-stat is high.
- E-769 Day-open recross follow: After the price spends a while on one side of the session open, a close back across it flips the day's winners and losers; follow the cross.
- E-770 Dry-volume new extreme fade: A new session high/low printed on well below usual volume has no real participation behind it; fade it.
- E-771 Impulse bar half-retrace resume: After an unusually large bar, a pullback to its midpoint that closes back past it shows the impulse holders are still in control; resume in the impulse direction.
- E-772 Sloping-VWAP pullback: When the session VWAP itself is clearly rising or falling, institutional average-price buyers/sellers defend it; the first touch that closes back on the trend side is a continuation entry.
- E-773 Accelerating-body momentum: Consecutive same-colour bars with growing bodies show urgency that has not yet been met by opposing flow; follow the acceleration.
- E-774 Prior-close location bias: A day that closed in the top (bottom) of its range leaves overnight/opening bias in that direction; in the morning, a cross of the prior close in that direction is followed.
- E-775 Hourly opening-range break: Order flow resets on the hour (scheduled data, desk shifts); a close beyond the first minutes' range of the hour is followed.
- E-776 Stall bar after a run fade: After a run, a bar with a much smaller range than the recent average shows the aggressive side has run out of fuel; fade the run.
- E-777 Higher-high higher-low staircase: A staircase of N bars each making both a higher high and a higher low (or lower/lower) is an orderly trend with no overlap for counter-traders; follow it.
- E-778 Minute-of-day seasonal sign: If the same minute of the day closed the same colour in most of the last 20 sessions, recurring scheduled flows (fixings, rebalancing, opening of other markets) at that clock time may repeat; follow the habit.
- E-779 Seasonal-path deviation fade: Compare the day's return from the open with the average path of the last 20 sessions at the same minute; a large excess over that typical path is overshoot against the usual flow, so fade it.
- E-780 Equal-close stall after a move: Bars closing exactly on the previous close show the aggressors stepping away after a move; the prior direction resumes once the pause passes.
- E-781 Session heavy-volume bar range break: The session's highest-volume bar so far marks where most positions were built; a close beyond its range leaves those holders offside and they must exit in the break direction.
- E-782 Price-impact spike fade: A move that needed little volume per point (impact far above the usual at that minute) is a liquidity vacuum, not conviction; it tends to retrace once liquidity returns.
- E-785 VWAP side at clock marks: At round clock marks, being clearly on one side of the session VWAP means the volume-weighted average holder is on the other side and the drift keeps that side; follow it.
- E-786 Tweezer reversal at a window extreme: Two consecutive bars stopping at the same high (low) after the first was strong marks a defended level at the window extreme; fade the second bar's reversal colour.
- E-787 Heavy-volume bar trap: A heavy-volume directional bar fully retraced by the next bar's close through its open traps the aggressors; the retrace direction continues.
- E-784 PTAX window move fade: Dollar flows around the four PTAX fixing windows (10, 11, 12, 13h) push the price temporarily; after the window ends the push is not information and tends to fade.
- E-788 Narrowest-bar break: The narrowest bar of the last n bars marks stalled quoting; the next bar closing beyond its extreme shows which side's resting orders were absorbed; follow.
- E-789 Stochastic re-entry fade: A close that leaves the top (bottom) of the recent range marks the end of the push by aggressive buyers (sellers); fade the exit from the extreme zone.
- E-790 Bar-open gap fade: A bar opening far from the previous close is a burst of market orders; if the bar then closes back against the burst, the aggressors are underwater; fade the gap.
- E-791 Rising-volume close stairs: Consecutive higher closes each on larger volume show participation growing with the move; follow once.
- E-792 Directional-movement balance cross: When upward bar extensions dominate downward ones over a window (Wilder directional movement), buyers are repeatedly pushing past prior highs; follow the crossing.
- E-793 Counter-trend shock bar fade: In a trending tape a large bar against the trend is often a liquidity sweep by a single large order; trade back with the trend.
- E-794 End-of-day midpoint pull: Late in the session, positions are squared toward the day's midpoint; price far from it in the last hour drifts back as day traders close.
- E-795 Two-bar midpoint reclaim: After a large bar, an opposite bar that retakes more than half of its body without exceeding its open shows the first move was rejected; trade the rejection.
- E-796 Volume dry-up then expansion: Three bars of very thin volume mean the book was left alone; the first bar with heavy volume shows the side that finally acts; follow its colour.
- E-797 Regression slope threshold cross: A least-squares line through recent closes that becomes steep enough shows persistent one-sided pressure rather than one noisy spike; follow the crossing.
- E-798 First-window return predicts late-day move: Intraday momentum: the first-window return of the session pushes institutional rebalancing and late hedging the same way; buy/sell when the last hours begin in the direction of the opening-window move.
- E-799 Return autocorrelation regime switch: When the rolling lag-1 autocorrelation of bar returns is strongly positive, a big bar is followed by more flow in its direction; when strongly negative, liquidity providers fade it. Trade the regime.
- E-800 Heavy-volume small-range absorption at the range edge: Big volume with a small range at the edge of the recent range means passive orders absorb the aggressors; trade away from the edge.
- E-801 Day-open recross follow: The session open is a reference many participants watch; after a long stay on one side, a close back across it flips the day's bias.
- E-802 Session-record range bar exhaustion fade: A bar with the widest range of the session that closes at its extreme is a climax where the last aggressors enter; fade it.
- E-803 Range-edge dwell continuation: Closes that stay in the top (bottom) fifth of the recent range for a while show persistent one-sided demand; follow when the dwell starts.
- E-804 Session midrange cross follow: Once the day has a wide range, the midpoint of high and low is the balance line; a close across it shows control moving to the other side.
- E-805 EMA stack alignment follow: When fast, medium and slow EMAs line up in order and price is beyond the fast one, trend followers across horizons agree; enter on the alignment.
- E-806 Regression t-statistic trend follow: A statistically strong linear trend over the window (high t-stat of price on time) marks a one-way flow; join when the t-stat first exceeds the bar.
- E-807 Kaufman efficiency-ratio surge follow: When price covers a window almost in a straight line (efficiency ratio high), little counter-flow exists; follow the direction on the first crossing.
- E-808 Lunch-range breakout: The thin midday hour builds a range of resting orders; a close beyond it after lunch shows the afternoon participants choosing a side, and stops beyond the range add fuel.
- E-809 Alternating-colour chop break: A string of alternating bar colours is two-sided balance; the first close beyond the whole chop range ends the balance and follows through.
- E-810 Keltner-envelope snapback: A close far outside an EMA envelope scaled by the average bar range is an overextension; the first bar back inside the envelope shows buyers/sellers of the extreme giving up.
- E-811 Chandelier-level recovery follow: After a pullback deeper than k average ranges from the window extreme, a close back above that trailing level means trend holders have defended it; follow the recovery.
- E-812 Persistent-body trend onset: When most of each bar's range is body over a window, moves are one-sided with little two-way trade; the onset of that state in the net direction continues.
- E-813 Previous-close reclaim: The previous settlement-like close is a reference for overnight positions; after a spell on one side, a close back across it shifts the day's bias.
- E-814 Close-line-break flip: A close beyond the extreme close of the last n bars flips a line-break trend state; the first flip shows the other side taking control.
- E-815 Volatility-onset direction follow: When recent average bar movement jumps above the hour's norm, new information is being priced; the direction of the burst keeps going while liquidity is being consumed.
- E-816 Thin volume-node crossing: Price entering a price bin where little volume traded today has few resting counterparties, so it tends to slide through to the next accepted area; follow the entry direction.
- E-817 Range-multiple thrust bar: A close that moves more than the previous bar's whole range beyond its close is a thrust that overwhelms the prior bar's liquidity; follow.
- E-828 Directional-streak exhaustion turn: After n consecutive bars each making a lower low (or higher high), the first bar that reverses with a higher high and higher close shows sellers (buyers) are spent; trade the turn.
- E-829 Open-drive bar follow: A larger-than-usual bar that opens at one extreme and closes at the other shows one side controlled the whole bar with no pullback; follow it.
- E-830 Floor-pivot R1/S1 rejection: Classic floor-trader pivots from the prior session are watched levels; a poke through R1/S1 that closes back inside is a failed push; fade it.
- E-831 RSI(2) dip inside an EMA trend: A very short-term oversold reading while price is above its EMA is a brief shakeout of weak hands in an uptrend; buy it (mirror for sells).
- E-832 Highest-volume bar follow: The bar with the highest volume of the window and a body over half its range shows aggressive one-sided participation; follow its direction.
- E-833 New closing high below VWAP fade: A rally to a new window closing high that is still below the session VWAP runs into volume-weighted supply from trapped longs; fade it (mirror for lows above VWAP).
- E-834 VWAP retest after a stretch: After price stretched far from VWAP, a pullback that touches VWAP and closes back on the trend side shows institutional buyers (sellers) defending their average price; follow.
- E-835 Two-bar swing failure: A bar that makes a new window extreme and is then fully engulfed by a close beyond its opposite end traps breakout traders; trade against the failed break.
- E-836 Multi-horizon momentum alignment: When short, medium and long horizon returns turn to the same sign with a long-horizon move above normal, participants of different horizons agree and the move should extend.
- E-837 Half-hour block return fade: Algorithms and desks rebalance on the half hour; a larger-than-usual move in the block just ended tends to be partly reversed as the new block starts.
- E-838 Round-number touch rejection: Resting orders cluster at round index/dollar levels; a bar that touches the nearest round level and closes back on the side it opened from means the level held, so trade away from it.
- E-839 Day-open reclaim follow: After trading on one side of the session open for a while, a close back across it shifts the intraday average holder into profit/loss and the move follows.
- E-840 Efficiency-ratio ignition follow: When Kaufman's efficiency ratio jumps above a threshold the tape moves in one direction with little noise; slower participants chase and the move continues.
- E-842 Heikin-Ashi colour flip after a run: A flip of smoothed candle colour after a run of the opposite colour marks a change of short-term control.
- E-843 Stochastic extreme exit follow: When the close leaves the bottom (top) zone of the recent range, sellers (buyers) are exhausted; trade the bounce away from the extreme.
- E-844 VWAP slope flip with side agreement: When the session VWAP changes slope and price sits on the same side, the average trade price is trending that way and money-flow follows.
- E-845 Rolling z-score stretch fade: Price stretched more than a few standard deviations from its recent mean is typically a liquidity-driven overshoot by impatient flow; fade it back.
- E-846 EMA cross with VWAP agreement: A fast/slow EMA cross while price is on the matching side of VWAP combines momentum and position vs average cost; follow the cross.
- E-847 Heavy-volume climax reversal bar: A very heavy bar that engulfs the previous bar against a prior move is capitulation of the trapped side; follow the reversal bar.
- E-848 Lunch-box break follow: The thin 12:00-13:00 box coils resting orders; the first close beyond it after 13:00 (until 14:30) releases stops and follows with the returning liquidity.
- E-849 Bar-colour breadth fade: When nearly all recent bars share one colour, the move is crowded and one-sided; the first threshold cross is a stretched sample and tends to revert.
- E-850 Open-price recross after a drive: After the day pushed away from its open by more than usual, a close back through the open price shows the drive's buyers or sellers are trapped; follow the recross.
- E-851 Index and dollar moving the same way: WIN and WDO usually move inversely; a large same-direction move in both is a risk-off/on anomaly or liquidity squeeze in one leg, and tends to snap back.
- E-852 Heavy-day breakout on the VWAP side: On a session whose cumulative volume runs above its usual pace, a new range extreme on the VWAP side comes with participation and continues.
- E-853 Bar-open jump fill: An unusual jump between one close and the next open is noise from thin books; a bar that trades back against the jump is a gap fill in progress.
- E-854 Range-position swing follow: A close that travels from the bottom (top) edge of the session range through its midpoint shows control changing sides; follow the crossing.
- E-856 Drift-excess follow: Return from the open in excess of the usual path for this time of day, measured against typical size, marks flow beyond the habitual drift; follow it.
- E-857 VWAP and prior-close confluence cross: Price clearing both the session VWAP and yesterday's close puts day-traders and swing holders on the same side; follow the first close past both.
- E-858 VWAP deviation z-score band fade: Price stretched several standard deviations from VWAP (measured on its own recent deviation) is pushed by impatient flow; liquidity providers fade it back toward VWAP.
- E-859 Bollinger squeeze expansion break: When band width is far below its usual level for the time of day, volatility is compressed; the first close outside the band signals the release direction.
- E-860 MACD histogram sign flip: A flip of the MACD histogram marks momentum changing hands between short and long horizon traders; follow the new sign.
- E-861 Donchian midpoint cross with rising mid: A close crossing the middle of the N-minute high/low channel while the channel midpoint already drifts the same way shows value migrating; follow it.
- E-862 Dollar lead, index lag: WIN and the dollar are inversely linked; when the dollar has moved much more than the index implies, index traders lag arbitrageurs and WIN catches up.
- E-863 EMA ribbon alignment onset: Close above a stack of rising-order EMAs (n, 2n, 4n) means all horizons agree; the first bar of alignment starts a trend leg.
- E-864 Late-session stretch back to VWAP: Near the close, day traders and funds flatten books; a large stretch from VWAP relative to the day's range tends to be pulled back as positions are squared.
- E-865 CCI extreme re-entry fade: When CCI falls back inside +-threshold after an extreme, the stretch has stopped extending and late momentum chasers are trapped; fade.
- E-866 DI cross with trend-strength filter: A +DI/-DI cross while directional movement is already strong (high ADX-like reading) indicates that the stronger side keeps control.
- E-867 Opening-range midpoint cross: After the opening range ends, the range midpoint is the balance point; a close crossing it shows the opening auction's winner and drives the day's side.
- E-868 Return-volume correlation follow: When bar returns and volumes correlate strongly positively over a window, volume arrives with the move (informed, aggressive flow); follow the net direction at the first crossing.
- E-869 Stale-close share break: A window with many unchanged closes means quoting is stale and the book is left alone; the first close beyond the window's range shows who finally acts; follow.
- E-870 Range-share thrust at a session extreme: When the last window covers most of the whole session range so far and sets a session extreme, one side has taken the day in one thrust with no balance; follow.
- E-871 Wick-chop regime stretch fade: Bar ranges much larger than the net bar-to-bar steps mean two-sided wicks: a mean-reverting regime where liquidity providers win; fade a 1.5 sigma stretch inside it.
- E-872 Hour-open recross follow: The price at the start of each clock hour is a reference for hourly algos and desks; after a spell on one side, a close back across it moves the average hourly participant to the other side; follow.
- E-873 Semi-variance dominance follow: When the squared up-moves dominate the squared down-moves in a window, the volatility is upside-driven (buyers pay up); follow the first crossing of the dominance threshold.
- E-874 Volume point-of-control magnet fade: The price bin with most volume today is the accepted value; price stretched far from it by impatient flow is pulled back as the auction rebalances; fade the stretch.
- E-875 Triple-touch ceiling/floor break: Three or more highs (lows) stopping at the same level inside a window show resting limit orders there; a close through it consumes them and releases stops; follow.
- E-876 Three soldiers/crows reversal from the wrong side of the EMA: Three strong, nested same-colour bodies that start from the far side of the EMA show a forced change of control after an exhausted move; trade the new direction.
- E-877 Tenkan/Kijun cross with price agreement: Midpoint of the short high/low channel crossing the midpoint of a 3x longer channel while price stays on that side shows short-horizon traders overtaking the slow ones (Ichimoku base lines); follow.
- E-878 Autocorrelation regime switch: When recent 1-bar returns are negatively autocorrelated, a large bar is noise from liquidity takers and reverts; when positively autocorrelated it is a continuing flow. Fade or follow by the measured sign.
- E-879 Low-overlap bar sequence follow: Three bars barely overlapping means one side takes every price level without giving any back; follow it.
- E-880 Volatility onset direction follow: When the short-window range jumps above its same-minute norm, informed flow has arrived; the net direction of the window continues for a while.
- E-881 Two-bar spring: A sizable bar that sweeps the previous bar's extreme and closes beyond its midpoint traps stop-loss orders on the wrong side; trade the reversal.
- E-882 Yesterday's range completion fade: Once today's range matches a fraction of yesterday's full range, new extremes that close back against their bar lose buyers/sellers; fade the poke.
- E-883 Leg retracement resume: After a clear leg, a pullback to a fraction of it that turns back is where trend traders re-enter; follow the resumption.
- E-884 Narrow-body cluster resolved by volume: A cluster of indecision bars followed by a full-body bar on higher volume shows resting orders have been consumed; follow the resolving bar.
- E-885 Heavy-volume day trend-side EMA follow: On a day with cumulative volume above its norm, the side of the day open carries real participation; follow closes on that side of a short EMA.
- E-886 Thin-book impact fade (Amihud): A bar with a large range per contract traded moved a thin book, not real demand; liquidity providers take the other side and price reverts.
- E-887 Volume-price correlation follow: When bar bodies and volume correlate strongly over a window, volume confirms direction (or its opposite for negative); follow a bar in that direction.
- E-888 Narrowest-range bar break: A bar with the smallest range of the last n bars marks compression; the next close beyond its extreme releases stored orders in that direction.
- E-889 Return acceleration follow: A move that is both large for the instrument and more than twice as fast as the previous equal window shows forced flow still arriving; follow it.
- E-890 Higher-high higher-low run: Several bars in a row that each raise both high and low (or lower both) show one-sided participation that has not met resistance; follow the onset.
- E-891 Volume spike confirmation follow: After a bar with volume far above normal, a next close beyond the spike bar's close confirms the aggressor won; follow.
- E-892 Late-session trend-side new extreme: In the final hour, fresh extremes in the direction of the day's drift come from institutions rebalancing into the close; follow.
- E-893 Prior clock-hour range break: The previous clock hour's high and low are levels watched by intraday traders; a close beyond them triggers stops and follow-through.
- E-894 Rising-volume directional run: Consecutive bars of the same colour each on more volume than the last show growing commitment, not exhaustion; follow the onset.
- E-895 Day-displacement sigma cross follow: When the day's move from the open exceeds a typical same-time move by k times, the day is trending and late participants chase it.
- E-896 Extreme recency tilt follow: If the session low is set much more recently than the session high, sellers gave up last; a close above a short EMA follows that tilt (and the reverse).
- E-897 Equity-open window range shock follow: Between 10:30 and 12:00 the US cash open brings foreign flow; a bar with a range far above normal and a strong close shows it, and the move continues.
- E-898 Rolling-median cross follow: The rolling median ignores single-bar spikes; a close crossing it marks a durable shift in where trades clear, which trend followers and stop orders then extend.
- E-899 Negative-autocorrelation regime fade: When recent bar returns alternate in sign (market making dominates), a large bar is mostly liquidity taking that gets reverted; fade it only in that regime.
- E-900 Staircase failure: After several consecutive higher highs and higher lows, a close below the previous low shows the trend buyers stopped; late trend followers exit into it and the move extends.
- E-901 Prior-close dwell cross: The previous session's close is the reference for overnight holders and gap traders; after dwelling on one side, a close across it signals a change of sentiment versus yesterday.
- E-902 Day-open re-cross after an excursion: After a stretch away from the open, a close back through it means the excursion was fully retraced and its late participants are trapped; follow the reversal.
- E-903 Absorption bar break: Heavy volume with a small range means one side absorbed the other's orders; the next bar's break of the absorption bar shows which side ran out.
- E-905 Force-index zero cross: Price change times volume weights moves by participation; the smoothed series changing sign marks money flow switching side and momentum traders joining.
- E-906 Reversal after range expansion: A bar several times wider than recent bars that closes near its extreme against the prior drift is a capitulation of the trend side; trapped positions fuel the reversal.
- E-907 One-sided bar-colour saturation fade: When nearly every recent bar has the same colour the move is crowded and thinly supplied by the other side; the first saturated reading precedes a pause or pullback, so fade it.
- E-904 Index surge, dollar fades (WDO): A sharp index move with dollar inversely correlated means foreign-flow hedgers lag; the dollar follows opposite to the index surge.
- E-908 VWAP sigma-band fade: Price far below/above VWAP in units of its own recent deviation is stretched; liquidity providers anchored to VWAP lean against it and price snaps back.
- E-909 EMA stretch in ATR units fade: A close several average bar-ranges away from a short EMA is an overextension from forced liquidation or chasing; fade the first bar that gets there.
- E-910 Bollinger band re-entry: A close outside the band followed by a close back inside shows the push was absorbed; trade back toward the mean.
- E-911 Volatility squeeze release: When recent dispersion is far below its typical level for this time of day, resting orders pile up on both sides; the first range break releases stops and continues.
- E-912 Staircase of higher lows: Consecutive bars each with higher low and higher close show persistent absorption of sellers; participants chasing keep it going.
- E-913 Dollar lead, index lag: WIN and WDO are negatively linked through foreign flow; after a large WDO move that WIN has not yet mirrored, the index catches up in the opposite direction.
- E-914 Minute-of-day seasonality: Scheduled flows (opening auctions, fixings, hedging) repeat at the same clock minute; follow the sign of the average next-bar return at this minute over the last 20 sessions when it is consistent.
- E-915 New-extreme clustering: Several fresh session highs (lows) inside a short window mean one side is repeatedly lifting the offer; stop-driven flow persists.
- E-916 Day-open stretch fade with reversal bar: When the price is far from the session open relative to the typical distance at this time, and a bar turns against it, late chasers are trapped; fade back toward the open.
- E-917 Wide reversal bar through prior body: A bar much wider than recent ones that closes near its extreme and through the previous opposite bar's open shows aggressive new flow overpowering the last move; follow it.
- E-918 Dollar leads index (inverse lag): WIN and WDO move inversely and the dollar futures often reprice first; when WDO moves unusually far over a short window while WIN has not yet moved against it, slower index participants catch up in the opposite direction.
- E-919 Bollinger band re-entry fade: A close outside a rolling 2-sigma band that closes back inside is an overshoot whose stop-driven buyers/sellers are exhausted; fade toward the mean.
- E-920 VWAP sigma-band re-entry fade: Session VWAP with the session's own deviation band marks where intraday inventory is stretched; a close back inside the band after an excursion means the push is spent.
- E-921 Donchian midpoint cross: The midpoint of the N-minute channel is the balance price; a close crossing it shows buyers or sellers taking control of the range.
- E-922 Chaikin money flow threshold cross: Volume-weighted close location over the window shows accumulation or distribution; crossing a threshold marks informed flow whose move should continue.
- E-923 Autocorrelation-regime big-bar trade: When recent bar returns are positively autocorrelated a large bar is followed, when negatively autocorrelated it is faded; the rolling regime selects the side.
- E-924 Three-EMA ribbon alignment onset: When price and three EMAs line up in order, short- and long-horizon traders agree on direction; the first bar of full alignment starts a leg.
- E-925 Compression then expansion bar: After a few bars much quieter than the last 30 minutes, a wide bar closing at its extreme shows resting liquidity was consumed; follow the release.
- E-926 Wide-day session midpoint cross: On days that have already ranged widely, the midpoint of the session range is the acceptance line; a close across it shows the day's control flipping.
- E-927 Index-vs-dollar ratio mean reversion: The log ratio of WIN to WDO is anchored by the common macro driver; a stretched ratio z-score means one leg overshot, and WIN tends to be the leg that reverts.

## Older rules (agent lane P-NNN and the pattern search): do not repeat these mechanisms

- P-013: WIN forced-liquidation cascade fade: trade the first counter bar after a 10-minute shock of 4x the trailing scale
- P-014: WIN abnormal-volume bar reversal: fade a 5-minute bar whose volume and move are both large for its time of day
- P-015: WIN same-slot periodicity: trade each half-hour slot in the sign of its own return over the previous 20 sessions
- P-016: WIN balanced-day VWAP reversion: fade 1.5-scale deviations from the session VWAP when the day has no trend and no volatility shock
- P-017: WIN stop-sweep failure fade: fade a 1-minute break of a standing 60-minute high/low that closes back inside within 5 bars
- P-018: WIN large-print flow continuation: follow a 5-minute bar whose contracts per print are unusually large for its time of day
- P-019: WIN dollar-unconfirmed move fade: fade a 30-minute WIN residual against the WDO-implied move on 1-minute bars
- P-020: WIN thin-volume move fade: fade a large 15-minute WIN move that came on no more than normal volume for its time of day
- P-021: WDO PTAX-window pressure fade: fade the 40-minute run-up into each of the four daily PTAX consultation windows
- P-022: WIN foreign-flow continuation: follow a 15-minute WIN move only when the dollar moves the opposite way at the same time
- P-023: WIN metaorder grind continuation: follow a 30-minute move built from many small same-sign minutes, no jump
- P-024: WIN absorption-bar fade: fade a 30-minute run when a 5-minute bar trades heavy volume but barely moves
- P-025: WIN auction-pressure reversal: fade the 20-minute impulse around the B3 cash open, the NYSE open and the cash closing call
- P-026: WIN compression-box breakout: follow a 5-minute close outside a 60-minute range that is tight for its time of day
- P-027: WIN prior-session level break: follow a 1-minute close through yesterday's high, low or close, stop back across the level
- P-028: WIN session autocorrelation switch: follow or fade 15-minute moves by the day's own variance ratio
- P-029: WIN low-volume-node traversal: follow a 1-minute exit from a high-volume price zone of today's profile into a thin zone
- P-030: WIN pullback against the multi-session trend: buy 30-minute dips in a 5-session up-trend, sell rallies in a down-trend
- P-031: WIN scheduled-information impulse follow: trade with the first 5 minutes after the 4 daily information times
